The other day at work, one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf, and I just yelled at her. And I mean, I’ve worked retail a long time, people make mistakes, shelves get messy all the time—especially with customers moving things around, you know? But afterward I was shaking, because that’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me.\n\nDoctor: Does this kind of irritability or anger happen in other parts of your life, like at home or with friends, or does it seem to only happen at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… work is where I really noticed it, because that’s where people are right in front of me and there are all these little mistakes happening. Like with the stock on the wrong shelf—I just blew up, completely over nothing, and then I was standing there shaking afterward because I don’t do that. Or I didn’t used to.\n\nAt home it’s… I don’t know, I guess I’m on edge too, but the big scary one was at work. Retail already makes you kind of tense sometimes—customers put things wherever, people call out, shipments come in wrong—but I’ve handled that for years. This felt different. Like my reaction was way bigger than the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting upset or angry easily at home or with friends, even if you haven’t been at work that day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I notice it most at work because that’s where everything is kind of in my face, you know? People, customers, shelves, the whole mess of it. But it’s like I’m quicker to snap in general lately.\n\nThat thing with my employee really shook me, because she just put stock on the wrong shelf. That’s not a big deal. We’ve all done it. Customers do it constantly. And I just… blew up at her like it was some huge thing. Afterward I was shaking, and I kept thinking, what is wrong with me, I don’t treat people like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or activities you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really been tracking that, honestly. Like, I still do things, but I’m kind of just getting through the day. I used to like wandering through home stores after work sometimes, which is funny because I work retail, so you’d think I’d want to be anywhere else, but I like looking at little kitchen things and candles and stuff.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I’ve lost interest exactly. I guess I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m enjoying things or just doing them because they’re part of my routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not getting as much enjoyment out of the things you used to like, such as wandering through home stores or other activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like… I still go sometimes, or I think about going, but then I get distracted by other stuff I have to do, or I’ll end up just wandering around and not really knowing what I’m looking for.\n\nIt’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying it or if my head is just somewhere else. Even with home stores, I’ll look at candles or little dishes, and then I’ll start thinking about whether the display is messy or how the employees must be dealing with people moving everything around, and then I’m not really shopping anymore. So… I don’t know. Maybe? But I haven’t clearly noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, like schedules and whether I forgot to order something, or—well, yesterday I spent ten minutes looking for my keys and they were in the refrigerator door, which sounds ridiculous, but I was putting away groceries and I guess set them there.\n\nBut anxious for no reason… I’m not sure. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “Oh, I’m anxious,” if that makes sense. Sometimes I’m just kind of wound up, but then there’s usually something I can point to, or I assume there is. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most days lately, even when things aren’t particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “hopeless” sounds so big, you know? I don’t think I’d use that word. Down, maybe, but then some days I’m just tired or irritated, and I’ll blame it on work or not sleeping right or the weather.\n\nIt’s hard because “most days” makes me feel like I should have been keeping a chart or something. I used to write things on sticky notes—like errands or shifts—and then they’d end up stuck to my steering wheel or inside my purse, and half the time I couldn’t remember why I wrote them. So with moods, I don’t really have a clean answer.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed myself sitting around feeling sad for no reason. It’s more like I’m just… getting through things, and then the day is over.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling irritable or easily annoyed even in situations where there isn’t much stress or pressure, like when you’re alone or doing something you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s actually part of why I’m here. I keep sort of… snapping at people over things that don’t deserve that kind of reaction.\n\nLike at work, one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf, and I just blew up at them. It wasn’t even a big thing—people put things in the wrong place all the time, and usually I’d just fix it or say something normal. But I yelled, and afterward I was shaking, like my whole body was still mad even though my brain knew it was too much.\n\nAnd then I started thinking about how the shelves were organized and whether customers had already moved things around, because they do that constantly, they’ll pick up a mug and put it in with the towels for no reason—sorry, I know that’s not the point. The point is, yes. I’m getting irritated way too fast, and it doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy most days, even when you haven’t been particularly busy or stressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’m tired, but who isn’t? Working retail, you’re on your feet and people ask you where something is while they’re standing right in front of it, and then I come home and there’s still laundry or dishes or whatever.\n\nBut “unusually” tired… I don’t know how to measure that. Some days I feel like I could just sit in the car for a while before going inside, but then I think maybe that’s normal after a long day. And sometimes I’m fine once I get moving. I haven’t really been tracking whether it’s most days or only certain days.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you were alone or in a calm situation, like watching TV or reading, where you still felt irritable or on edge, even though there was nothing stressful happening around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think so. It’s not always some big dramatic thing happening around me. Sometimes I’ll just be sitting there and feel like I’m already braced for somebody to do something wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nBut the clearest thing is still at work, honestly. I know you asked about being alone, and I’m trying to answer that, but that moment with the shelf keeps coming back to me because it was so stupid—one of my employees put stock in the wrong place, and I just… blew up. Yelled at them. Over basically nothing.\n\nAnd afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t caught up with the fact that it wasn’t a real problem. I kept thinking, why did I do that? That’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me a little, because it felt like I went from zero to furious way too fast.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt angry or irritable when nothing particularly frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know I keep coming back to the same example, but that’s the one that really bothered me. I’m here because I keep blowing up at people over things that are just… not worth blowing up about.\n\nLike with one of my employees—she put stock on the wrong shelf, which happens all the time. Customers move things around too, and honestly half the store is just us fixing where people put stuff back wrong. But I yelled at her. Really yelled. And afterward I was shaking, like my body was still in some fight even though nothing serious had happened.\n\nThat’s what scares me. It’s not how I usually treat people, and it felt like it came out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little interest or pleasure in doing things for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “nearly every day.” That sounds very specific, and I haven’t really been keeping score like that.\n\nI mean, there are things I don’t feel like doing lately, but then again after work sometimes I never feel like doing anything except sitting down. Like I’ll think, oh, I should watch that show, or call someone back, or go look at something at the store that isn’t my store for once—then I just don’t. But I don’t know if that’s “little interest” or just being tired or distracted.\n\nAnd “most of the day”… I’m not sure. My days are kind of chopped up around shifts and errands, so it’s hard to tell. I guess I haven’t noticed it as a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you really didn’t feel like doing anything, even things you usually enjoy, for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, there are days I come home and I don’t want to do anything, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not enjoying things. Sometimes I’ll put the TV on and not really watch it, but then I do that even when I’m just tired.\n\nIt’s hard because my schedule’s all over the place, and then I start thinking about chores or the store or whether I forgot to text somebody back, and suddenly the whole evening is gone. Like, I used to like wandering around Target—well, not my store, obviously, because then I start straightening shelves without meaning to—but lately even that sounds like too much effort sometimes.\n\nBut “most of the day,” I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed it in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt irritable or snapped at someone even though there wasn’t any real stress or frustration going on at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Yelled at them. Over basically nothing.\n\nAnd afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t caught up with the fact that it wasn’t a real problem. I kept thinking, why did I do that? That’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me a little, because it felt like I went from zero to furious way too fast.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt angry or irritable when nothing particularly frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know I keep coming back to the same example, but that’s the one that really bothered me. It’s not how I usually treat people, and it felt like it came out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little interest or pleasure in doing things for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “nearly every day.” That sounds very specific, and I haven’t really been keeping score like that.\n\nI mean, there are things I don’t feel like doing lately, but then again after work sometimes I never feel like doing anything except sitting down. Like I’ll think, oh, I should watch that show, or call someone back, or go look at something at the store that isn’t my store for once—then I just don’t. But I don’t know if that’s “little interest” or just being tired or distracted.\n\nAnd “most of the day”… I’m not sure. My days are kind of chopped up around shifts and errands, so it’s hard to tell. I guess I haven’t noticed it as a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you really didn’t feel like doing anything, even things you usually enjoy, for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, I used to like wandering around Target—well, not my store, obviously, because then I start straightening shelves without meaning to—but lately even that sounds like too much effort sometimes.\n\nBut “most of the day,” I don’t know. I’m sorry, I know you’re asking for a calm, alone moment, and I keep not giving you exactly that. I can’t think of a perfect example like I was sitting quietly and suddenly got mad.

I haven’t really been tracking whether it’s most days or only certain days.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you were alone or in a calm situation, like watching TV or reading, where you still felt irritable or on edge, even though there was nothing stressful happening around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think so. It’s not always some big dramatic thing happening around me. Sometimes I’ll just be sitting there and feel like I’m already braced for somebody to do something wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nBut the clearest thing is still at work, honestly. I know you asked about being alone, and I’m trying to answer that, but that moment with the shelf keeps coming back to me because it was so stupid—one of my employees put stock in the wrong place, and I just… blew up. Yelled at them. Over basically nothing.\n\nAnd afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t caught up with the fact that it wasn’t a real problem. I kept thinking, why did I do that? That’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me a little, because it felt like I went from zero to furious way too fast.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt angry or irritable when nothing particularly frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know I keep coming back to the same example, but that’s the one that really bothered me. I’m here because I keep blowing up at people over things that are just… not worth blowing up about.\n\nLike with one of my employees—she put stock on the wrong shelf, which happens all the time. Customers move things around too, and honestly half the store is just us fixing where people put stuff back wrong. But I yelled at her. Really yelled. And afterward I was shaking, like my body was still in some fight even though nothing serious had happened.\n\nThat’s what scares me. It’s not how I usually treat people, and it felt like it came out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little interest or pleasure in doing things for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “nearly every day.” That sounds very specific, and I haven’t really been keeping score like that.\n\nI mean, there are things I don’t feel like doing lately, but then again after work sometimes I never feel like doing anything except sitting down. Like I’ll think, oh, I should watch that show, or call someone back, or go look at something at the store that isn’t my store for once—then I just don’t. But I don’t know if that’s “little interest” or just being tired or distracted.\n\nAnd “most of the day”… I’m not sure. My days are kind of chopped up around shifts and errands, so it’s hard to tell. I guess I haven’t noticed it as a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you really didn’t feel like doing anything, even things you usually enjoy, for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, there are days I come home and I don’t want to do anything, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not enjoying things. Sometimes I’ll put the TV on and not really watch it, but then I do that even when I’m just tired.\n\nIt’s hard because my schedule’s all over the place, and then I start thinking about chores or the store or whether I forgot to text somebody back, and suddenly the whole evening is gone. Like, I used to like wandering around Target—well, not my store, obviously, because then I start straightening shelves without meaning to—but lately even that sounds like too much effort sometimes.\n\nBut “most of the day,” I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed it in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt irritable or snapped at someone even though there wasn’t any real stress or frustration going on at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, that’s kind of the reason I’m here, honestly. I keep snapping at people over things that are… nothing, really.\n\nLike the employee I mentioned—she put stock on the wrong shelf, and that happens all the time in retail. Half the day is fixing where things go anyway. But I just went off on her. I yelled, and it was way bigger than the situation deserved. And then afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t gotten the message that it was just a shelf and some misplaced stock.\n\nI keep thinking about it because I’m not usually like that. I don’t want to be that kind of boss, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or sad for no clear reason, even when things around you aren’t particularly stressful or upsetting?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. “Down or sad” is hard for me to pin down, because I wouldn’t say I’m walking around crying or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of flat, maybe, but then I’ll realize I skipped lunch or I’ve been standing under those awful store lights for eight hours, and honestly those lights could make anyone feel weird. We had this one bulb flickering near the seasonal aisle for weeks, and I kept telling people it was going to drive me insane—not literally, but you know what I mean.\n\nSo… for no clear reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed sadness just showing up like that. It’s more that I get caught up in whatever’s going on and then later I think, why did I feel so off? But I don’t know if that’s sad exactly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt angry or irritable even though nothing frustrating or upsetting was happening around you at all—like when you were by yourself, doing something routine, or in a calm environment?\n\nPatient: I mean… the clearest thing is still what happened at work. I know you’re asking about when I’m alone or calm, but that’s what keeps coming to mind, because it was so out of proportion.\n\nShe put stock on the wrong shelf, which is such a normal thing—like, honestly, if I had a dollar for every item that ended up in the wrong spot, I could quit retail entirely. And I just… blew up at her. I yelled. Over nothing. And afterward I was shaking, which made me feel even worse, because that is not how I usually treat people.\n\nWhen I’m by myself, I don’t know if I’d call it the same thing. But I do get this angry feeling sitting there sometimes, like my body’s already mad before anything even happens. Then something tiny sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt angry or irritable even though absolutely nothing frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something you normally find calming?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something where I was just alone and calm, and honestly the one that keeps coming up is still the work thing. It’s not like she broke something expensive or cursed at a customer. And I just snapped. I yelled at her like it was this huge deal, and it wasn’t.\n\nAfterward I was shaking, and I remember thinking, what is wrong with me? Because I don’t usually treat people like that. I don’t want people walking around the store afraid I’m going to explode over a shelf tag, you know?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt angry or irritable even though absolutely nothing frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something you normally find calming?\n\nPatient: I know I’m kind of circling the same example, but that’s honestly why I’m here. I know you keep asking “completely alone,” and I’m not trying to avoid it, I just… I can’t think of a clean example like that.

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I keep… snapping, I guess. Like, blowing up at people over really small things. The other day at work, one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf, and I just yelled at her. And I mean, I’ve worked retail a long time, people make mistakes, shelves get messy all the time—especially with customers moving things around, you know? But afterward I was shaking, because that’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me.\n\nDoctor: Does this kind of irritability or anger happen in other parts of your life, like at home or with friends, or does it seem to only happen at work?\n\nPatient: I mean… work is where I really noticed it, because that’s where people are right in front of me and there are all these little mistakes happening. Like with the stock on the wrong shelf—I just blew up, completely over nothing, and then I was standing there shaking afterward because I don’t do that. Or I didn’t used to.\n\nAt home it’s… I don’t know, I guess I’m on edge too, but the big scary one was at work. Retail already makes you kind of tense sometimes—customers put things wherever, people call out, shipments come in wrong—but I’ve handled that for years. This felt different. Like my reaction was way bigger than the problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself getting upset or angry easily at home or with friends, even if you haven’t been at work that day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I notice it most at work because that’s where everything is kind of in my face, you know? People, customers, shelves, the whole mess of it. But it’s like I’m quicker to snap in general lately.\n\nThat thing with my employee really shook me, because she just put stock on the wrong shelf. That’s not a big deal. We’ve all done it. Customers do it constantly. And I just… blew up at her like it was some huge thing. Afterward I was shaking, and I kept thinking, what is wrong with me, I don’t treat people like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or activities you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really been tracking that, honestly. Like, I still do things, but I’m kind of just getting through the day. I used to like wandering through home stores after work sometimes, which is funny because I work retail, so you’d think I’d want to be anywhere else, but I like looking at little kitchen things and candles and stuff.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I’ve lost interest exactly. I guess I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m enjoying things or just doing them because they’re part of my routine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not getting as much enjoyment out of the things you used to like, such as wandering through home stores or other activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like… I still go sometimes, or I think about going, but then I get distracted by other stuff I have to do, or I’ll end up just wandering around and not really knowing what I’m looking for.\n\nIt’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying it or if my head is just somewhere else. Even with home stores, I’ll look at candles or little dishes, and then I’ll start thinking about whether the display is messy or how the employees must be dealing with people moving everything around, and then I’m not really shopping anymore. So… I don’t know. Maybe? But I haven’t clearly noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, like schedules and whether I forgot to order something, or—well, yesterday I spent ten minutes looking for my keys and they were in the refrigerator door, which sounds ridiculous, but I was putting away groceries and I guess set them there.\n\nBut anxious for no reason… I’m not sure. I haven’t been sitting there thinking, “Oh, I’m anxious,” if that makes sense. Sometimes I’m just kind of wound up, but then there’s usually something I can point to, or I assume there is. I don’t know if that counts.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most days lately, even when things aren’t particularly stressful?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “hopeless” sounds so big, you know? I don’t think I’d use that word. Down, maybe, but then some days I’m just tired or irritated, and I’ll blame it on work or not sleeping right or the weather.\n\nIt’s hard because “most days” makes me feel like I should have been keeping a chart or something. I used to write things on sticky notes—like errands or shifts—and then they’d end up stuck to my steering wheel or inside my purse, and half the time I couldn’t remember why I wrote them. So with moods, I don’t really have a clean answer.\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed myself sitting around feeling sad for no reason. It’s more like I’m just… getting through things, and then the day is over.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling irritable or easily annoyed even in situations where there isn’t much stress or pressure, like when you’re alone or doing something you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s actually part of why I’m here. I keep sort of… snapping at people over things that don’t deserve that kind of reaction.\n\nLike at work, one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf, and I just blew up at them. It wasn’t even a big thing—people put things in the wrong place all the time, and usually I’d just fix it or say something normal. But I yelled, and afterward I was shaking, like my whole body was still mad even though my brain knew it was too much.\n\nAnd then I started thinking about how the shelves were organized and whether customers had already moved things around, because they do that constantly, they’ll pick up a mug and put it in with the towels for no reason—sorry, I know that’s not the point. The point is, yes. I’m getting irritated way too fast, and it doesn’t feel like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy most days, even when you haven’t been particularly busy or stressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’m tired, but who isn’t? Working retail, you’re on your feet and people ask you where something is while they’re standing right in front of it, and then I come home and there’s still laundry or dishes or whatever.\n\nBut “unusually” tired… I don’t know how to measure that. Some days I feel like I could just sit in the car for a while before going inside, but then I think maybe that’s normal after a long day. And sometimes I’m fine once I get moving. I haven’t really been tracking whether it’s most days or only certain days.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you were alone or in a calm situation, like watching TV or reading, where you still felt irritable or on edge, even though there was nothing stressful happening around you?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, I think so. It’s not always some big dramatic thing happening around me. Sometimes I’ll just be sitting there and feel like I’m already braced for somebody to do something wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nBut the clearest thing is still at work, honestly. I know you asked about being alone, and I’m trying to answer that, but that moment with the shelf keeps coming back to me because it was so stupid—one of my employees put stock in the wrong place, and I just… blew up. Yelled at them. Over basically nothing.\n\nAnd afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t caught up with the fact that it wasn’t a real problem. I kept thinking, why did I do that? That’s not how I usually treat people. It scared me a little, because it felt like I went from zero to furious way too fast.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt angry or irritable when nothing particularly frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know I keep coming back to the same example, but that’s the one that really bothered me. I’m here because I keep blowing up at people over things that are just… not worth blowing up about.\n\nLike with one of my employees—she put stock on the wrong shelf, which happens all the time. Customers move things around too, and honestly half the store is just us fixing where people put stuff back wrong. But I yelled at her. Really yelled. And afterward I was shaking, like my body was still in some fight even though nothing serious had happened.\n\nThat’s what scares me. It’s not how I usually treat people, and it felt like it came out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt little interest or pleasure in doing things for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “nearly every day.” That sounds very specific, and I haven’t really been keeping score like that.\n\nI mean, there are things I don’t feel like doing lately, but then again after work sometimes I never feel like doing anything except sitting down. Like I’ll think, oh, I should watch that show, or call someone back, or go look at something at the store that isn’t my store for once—then I just don’t. But I don’t know if that’s “little interest” or just being tired or distracted.\n\nAnd “most of the day”… I’m not sure. My days are kind of chopped up around shifts and errands, so it’s hard to tell. I guess I haven’t noticed it as a clear pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you had days where you really didn’t feel like doing anything, even things you usually enjoy, for most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, there are days I come home and I don’t want to do anything, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not enjoying things. Sometimes I’ll put the TV on and not really watch it, but then I do that even when I’m just tired.\n\nIt’s hard because my schedule’s all over the place, and then I start thinking about chores or the store or whether I forgot to text somebody back, and suddenly the whole evening is gone. Like, I used to like wandering around Target—well, not my store, obviously, because then I start straightening shelves without meaning to—but lately even that sounds like too much effort sometimes.\n\nBut “most of the day,” I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed it in a clear way.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt irritable or snapped at someone even though there wasn’t any real stress or frustration going on at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean, that’s kind of the reason I’m here, honestly. I keep snapping at people over things that are… nothing, really.\n\nLike the employee I mentioned—she put stock on the wrong shelf, and that happens all the time in retail. Half the day is fixing where things go anyway. But I just went off on her. I yelled, and it was way bigger than the situation deserved. And then afterward I was shaking, like my body hadn’t gotten the message that it was just a shelf and some misplaced stock.\n\nI keep thinking about it because I’m not usually like that. I don’t want to be that kind of boss, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or sad for no clear reason, even when things around you aren’t particularly stressful or upsetting?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. “Down or sad” is hard for me to pin down, because I wouldn’t say I’m walking around crying or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel kind of flat, maybe, but then I’ll realize I skipped lunch or I’ve been standing under those awful store lights for eight hours, and honestly those lights could make anyone feel weird. We had this one bulb flickering near the seasonal aisle for weeks, and I kept telling people it was going to drive me insane—not literally, but you know what I mean.\n\nSo… for no clear reason? I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed sadness just showing up like that. It’s more that I get caught up in whatever’s going on and then later I think, why did I feel so off? But I don’t know if that’s sad exactly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt angry or irritable even though nothing frustrating or upsetting was happening around you at all—like when you were by yourself, doing something routine, or in a calm environment?\n\nPatient: I mean… the clearest thing is still what happened at work. I know you’re asking about when I’m alone or calm, but that’s what keeps coming to mind, because it was so out of proportion.\n\nShe put stock on the wrong shelf, which is such a normal thing—like, honestly, if I had a dollar for every item that ended up in the wrong spot, I could quit retail entirely. And I just… blew up at her. I yelled. Over nothing. And afterward I was shaking, which made me feel even worse, because that is not how I usually treat people.\n\nWhen I’m by myself, I don’t know if I’d call it the same thing. But I do get this angry feeling sitting there sometimes, like my body’s already mad before anything even happens. Then something tiny sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt angry or irritable even though absolutely nothing frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something you normally find calming?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something where I was just alone and calm, and honestly the one that keeps coming up is still the work thing. I know that’s not exactly what you’re asking, but it’s the reason I started worrying about myself.\n\nShe put stock on the wrong shelf, which is just… that’s retail. That happens constantly. It’s not like she broke something expensive or cursed at a customer. And I just snapped. I yelled at her like it was this huge deal, and it wasn’t.\n\nAfterward I was shaking, and I remember thinking, what is wrong with me? Because I don’t usually treat people like that. I don’t want people walking around the store afraid I’m going to explode over a shelf tag, you know?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a specific time when you felt angry or irritable even though absolutely nothing frustrating or stressful was happening around you—like when you were alone, resting, or doing something you normally find calming?\n\nPatient: I know I’m kind of circling the same example, but that’s honestly why I’m here. I don’t have a clean “I was alone on the couch and suddenly angry” story in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I keep blowing up at people over nothing. Like with one of my employees—she put stock on the wrong shelf, which is not some major disaster, it’s just retail, it happens all day long. And I yelled at her. I really yelled. Then afterward I was shaking, and I felt awful because that is not how I usually treat people.\n\nSo if you mean “for no good reason,” then yes, that’s the kind of thing I mean. It feels like I’m already wound up, and then something tiny becomes this huge thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt angry or irritable when you were alone or in a calm situation, with no real stress or frustration happening around you at that moment?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re asking for a calm, alone moment, and I keep not giving you exactly that. I can’t think of a perfect example like I was sitting quietly and suddenly got mad.\n\nWhat I can tell you is I’m here because I keep blowing up at people over things that shouldn’t matter. Like one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf—just normal retail stuff, not a crisis—and I yelled at her. Really yelled. And afterward I was shaking, like my whole body was still in it, and I just kept thinking, this is not me. This isn’t how I usually treat people.\n\nSo maybe it’s not happening with absolutely nothing around me, but it feels like there’s no real reason for how angry I get.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt angry or irritable even when you were completely alone and nothing around you was stressful or frustrating?\n\nPatient: I know you keep asking “completely alone,” and I’m not trying to avoid it, I just… I can’t think of a clean example like that.\n\nIt’s more that I’m here because I keep blowing up at people over practically nothing. Like at work, one of my employees put stock on the wrong shelf, and that’s so normal, I mean, half the store is always getting rearranged anyway and we’re short on time and—sorry. The point is, it wasn’t a big deal. But I yelled at her. Really yelled.\n\nAnd afterward I was shaking, because that is not how I usually treat people. It scared me, honestly. So not alone with nothing happening, no, but the anger is way bigger than the situation."
